Sometimes, yeah… not constantly, but it can happen outside work too, I think. Like if I’m talking to someone and trying to explain a simple thing, I’ll start in one place and then my mind sort of grabs onto another piece of it, and then another.

I know what I’m trying to get at, mostly, but it doesn’t always come out in the right order. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that others around you didn’t seem to understand or agree with, even when there was no clear reason for you to think that way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like believing something and nobody could talk me out of it.\n\nI mean, I can be stubborn sometimes, especially if I think I’m right about something at work, but that’s more just normal disagreement, I think. I haven’t had some strong belief where everyone else is saying there’s no reason for it. At least… not that I’ve noticed. Does that sound right?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something very strongly, even when others couldn’t see any clear reason for it or tried to tell you it wasn’t true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had that, no. I mean, I’ve been sure I was right about regular things, like work stuff, and maybe I didn’t explain it well… but not where people were all telling me something wasn’t true and I just kept believing it anyway.\n\nAt least not that I can think of. If I’m missing what you mean, you can tell me, but I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Outside of explaining things at work, do you ever find your thoughts or speech becoming jumbled or hard to follow, even when you're just talking to friends or thinking to yourself?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah… not constantly, but it can happen outside work too, I think. Like if I’m talking to someone and trying to explain a simple thing, I’ll start in one place and then my mind sort of grabs onto another piece of it, and then another.\n\nIt’s a little embarrassing to say. I’ll think I’m being clear, but then I can tell by their face that I’ve kind of lost them. It’s the same sort of thing as with the inventory or the schedule—like trucks, then Thursday, then some other detail that feels connected to me at the time. I don’t mean to ramble. I’m trying to answer you right.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. If something seemed odd, I’d usually check myself or ask somebody. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things, or feeling sensations that others don’t seem to notice, even outside of work and not just when you’re explaining something?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes if I’m tired I might think I heard my name or something, but then it’s usually just background noise or me not paying attention.\n\nI haven’t been seeing things or feeling things that other people don’t. At least not that I’ve noticed. I’m trying to be honest, but I don’t want to make something out of nothing, if that makes sense.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave there been times when you suddenly lose your train of thought completely, like the words just vanish and you can’t remember what you were saying or thinking?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\nmanifestation: \"I came in because when I try to explain inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, my words start jumping from trucks to Thursdays to the church grill to my boss’s tone, and people look at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… sometimes, yes. I don’t know if it’s that the words completely vanish, exactly, but I’ll be trying to explain something at work, like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind sort of jumps around.

I’ll start with the trucks, then somehow I’m talking about Thursday, then the church grill, then my boss’s tone… and I can tell people are looking at me like they’re not following. I usually try to pull it back and make it sound clearer, but it’s embarrassing.

So yes, I guess there are times I lose the thread. I hope that’s the kind of thing you mean.

No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that. Not someone controlling my thoughts or me having some special connection to things.

I’m trying to be honest, but I don’t want to make something out of nothing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you suddenly lose your train of thought completely, like the words just vanish and you can’t remember what you were saying or thinking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, yes. I don’t know if it’s that the words completely vanish, exactly, but I’ll be trying to explain something at work, like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind sort of jumps around.\n\nI’ll start with the trucks, then somehow I’m talking about Thursday, then the church grill, then my boss’s tone… and I can tell people are looking at me like they’re not following. I usually try to pull it back and make it sound clearer, but it’s embarrassing.\n\nSo yes, I guess there are times I lose the thread. I hope that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would find very strange or impossible, even if just for a little while—like thinking someone was controlling your thoughts, or that you had a special connection to events that others couldn’t see?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that. Not someone controlling my thoughts or me having some special connection to things.\n\nI mean, I can get caught up in trying to understand why something happened, or I might take something personally and then realize later I was probably just stressed. But I don’t think I’ve believed something impossible, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI’m trying not to minimize it, but I also don’t want to say yes to something that doesn’t really fit.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever notice your thoughts or speech becoming jumbled or hard to follow when you're not at work, like when you're talking to family or just thinking through something by yourself?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 59 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / bachelors\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Disorganized_Thinking_Speech\nmanifestation: \"I came in because when I try to explain inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, my words start jumping from trucks to Thursdays to the church grill to my boss’s tone, and people look at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
A little bit, yeah… not as much as at work, I don’t think, or maybe I just notice it more there because people are waiting on me to be clear.

But even with family, sometimes I’ll be trying to explain something simple and I can feel myself sort of bouncing around. Like I start on one point and then I’m suddenly on another, and I can tell I’m not making it easy to follow.

That’s actually part of why I came in, I guess. At work especially, when I’m trying to explain inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, my words start jumping from trucks to Thursdays to the church grill to my boss’s tone… and people look at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. I just lose the order of it sometimes.

Yes… I mean, I think so, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.

Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things, or feeling sensations that others don’t seem to notice, even outside of work and not just when you’re explaining something?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes if I’m tired I might think I heard my name or something, but then it’s usually just background noise or me not paying attention.\n\nI haven’t been seeing things or feeling things that other people don’t. I’m trying to be honest, but I don’t want to make something out of nothing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you suddenly lose your train of thought completely, like the words just vanish and you can’t remember what you were saying or thinking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, yes. I’ll think I’m being clear, but then I can tell by their face that I’ve kind of lost them. It’s the same sort of thing as with the inventory or the schedule—like trucks, then Thursday, then some other detail that feels connected to me at the time. I don’t mean to ramble. I’m trying to answer you right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or broadcasted so others can hear them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had anything like that. I mean, my thoughts can feel busy or kind of hard to organize sometimes, like I said, but not like someone else is putting them there or taking them out.\n\nAnd I don’t think people can hear them or anything like that. That would be… no, I haven’t noticed that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that other people might find very strange or impossible, like thinking that you have special powers, that you're receiving secret messages, or that something is happening that isn't really happening?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… I’ve had strong opinions, maybe, or I’ve read into something and then later realized I was probably overthinking it, but not like secret messages or special powers.\n\nI don’t want to give the wrong answer, but as far as I know, I haven’t believed anything impossible like that. If something seemed odd, I’d usually check myself or ask somebody. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things, or feeling sensations that others don’t seem to notice, even outside of work and not just when you’re explaining something?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes if I’m tired I might think I heard my name or something, but then it’s usually just background noise or me not paying attention.\n\nI haven’t been seeing things or feeling things that other people don’t. At least not that I’ve noticed. I’m trying to be honest, but I don’t want to make something out of nothing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you suddenly lose your train of thought completely, like the words just vanish and you can’t remember what you were saying or thinking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, yes. I don’t know if it’s that the words completely vanish, exactly, but I’ll be trying to explain something at work, like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind sort of jumps around.\n\nI’ll start with the trucks, then somehow I’m talking about Thursday, then the church grill, then my boss’s tone… and I can tell people are looking at me like they’re not following. I hope that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would find very strange or impossible, even if just for a little while—like thinking someone was controlling your thoughts, or that you had a special connection to events that others couldn’t see?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that. But I don’t think I’ve believed something impossible, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI’m trying not to minimize it, but I also don’t want to say yes to something that doesn’t really fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice your thoughts or speech becoming jumbled or hard to follow when you're not at work, like when you're talking to family or just thinking through something by yourself?\n\nPatient: A little bit, yeah… not as much as at work, I don’t think, or maybe I just notice it more there because people are waiting on me to be clear.\n\nBut even with family, sometimes I’ll be trying to explain something simple and I can feel myself sort of bouncing around. Not exactly like my mind goes completely blank, no… or at least I wouldn’t put it that strongly.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23.1, F23.2, F23.3, F23.8, F23.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I, uh… I’m hoping it’s not too big a thing, but at work I’ve been having some trouble getting my words to come out straight. I supervise retail sales, and when I try to explain things like inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, I’ll start in one place and then suddenly I’m talking about trucks, or Thursday, or the church grill, or the way my boss said something… and people just kind of look at me like they can’t follow.\n\nI don’t mean to waste anyone’s time. I just thought maybe I should ask about it, if that’s all right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or broadcasted so others can hear them?\n\nPatient: Um… no, I don’t think so. Not like that, anyway. I haven’t noticed thoughts being put in or taken out, and I don’t think other people can hear them. I mean, I do get tangled up in what I’m trying to say sometimes, but that’s different, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things that others around you don’t seem to notice—like hearing voices, seeing things, or feeling sensations that aren’t there?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think so, no. I mean, I haven’t been seeing things or hearing voices, not that I’ve noticed. Sometimes I might mishear something in a busy store, but I think that’s just noise and people talking over each other. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that seem very strange or unusual, like you're being watched, followed, or that something is happening that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a serious way. I mean, sometimes at work I feel like people are watching me a bit when I’m not explaining myself well, but that seems normal, doesn’t it? Like they’re just waiting for me to get to the point.\n\nI haven’t felt followed or anything like that. And I don’t think there’s some special thing happening that other people don’t understand. At least, nothing I’d want to make too much of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that didn't seem to make sense, or that you later realized were completely off track, like something wasn't quite right with the way you were putting things together?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess that’s kind of why I came in, actually. I don’t mean that I’m, you know, completely not making sense all the time. But when I try to explain things at work—like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule—I start out okay, and then my words sort of jump around.\n\nLike I’ll be talking about trucks, and then somehow I’m on Thursday, and then the church grill, and then my boss’s tone… and I can see people looking at me like they can’t quite follow what I’m saying. I know what I’m trying to get at, mostly, but it doesn’t always come out in the right order. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something very strongly that others around you didn’t seem to understand or agree with, even when there was no clear reason for you to think that way?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like believing something and nobody could talk me out of it.\n\nI mean, I can be stubborn sometimes, especially if I think I’m right about something at work, but that’s more just normal disagreement, I think. I haven’t had some strong belief where everyone else is saying there’s no reason for it. At least… not that I’ve noticed. Does that sound right?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something very strongly, even when others couldn’t see any clear reason for it or tried to tell you it wasn’t true?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve had that, no. I mean, I’ve been sure I was right about regular things, like work stuff, and maybe I didn’t explain it well… but not where people were all telling me something wasn’t true and I just kept believing it anyway.\n\nAt least not that I can think of. If I’m missing what you mean, you can tell me, but I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Outside of explaining things at work, do you ever find your thoughts or speech becoming jumbled or hard to follow, even when you're just talking to friends or thinking to yourself?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah… not constantly, but it can happen outside work too, I think. Like if I’m talking to someone and trying to explain a simple thing, I’ll start in one place and then my mind sort of grabs onto another piece of it, and then another.\n\nIt’s a little embarrassing to say. I’ll think I’m being clear, but then I can tell by their face that I’ve kind of lost them. It’s the same sort of thing as with the inventory or the schedule—like trucks, then Thursday, then some other detail that feels connected to me at the time. I don’t mean to ramble. I’m trying to answer you right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or broadcasted so others can hear them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had anything like that. I mean, my thoughts can feel busy or kind of hard to organize sometimes, like I said, but not like someone else is putting them there or taking them out.\n\nAnd I don’t think people can hear them or anything like that. That would be… no, I haven’t noticed that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any beliefs or ideas that other people might find very strange or impossible, like thinking that you have special powers, that you're receiving secret messages, or that something is happening that isn't really happening?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. I mean… I’ve had strong opinions, maybe, or I’ve read into something and then later realized I was probably overthinking it, but not like secret messages or special powers.\n\nI don’t want to give the wrong answer, but as far as I know, I haven’t believed anything impossible like that. If something seemed odd, I’d usually check myself or ask somebody. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things, or feeling sensations that others don’t seem to notice, even outside of work and not just when you’re explaining something?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes if I’m tired I might think I heard my name or something, but then it’s usually just background noise or me not paying attention.\n\nI haven’t been seeing things or feeling things that other people don’t. At least not that I’ve noticed. I’m trying to be honest, but I don’t want to make something out of nothing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you suddenly lose your train of thought completely, like the words just vanish and you can’t remember what you were saying or thinking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, yes. I don’t know if it’s that the words completely vanish, exactly, but I’ll be trying to explain something at work, like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind sort of jumps around.\n\nI’ll start with the trucks, then somehow I’m talking about Thursday, then the church grill, then my boss’s tone… and I can tell people are looking at me like they’re not following. I usually try to pull it back and make it sound clearer, but it’s embarrassing.\n\nSo yes, I guess there are times I lose the thread. I hope that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would find very strange or impossible, even if just for a little while—like thinking someone was controlling your thoughts, or that you had a special connection to events that others couldn’t see?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that. Not someone controlling my thoughts or me having some special connection to things.\n\nI mean, I can get caught up in trying to understand why something happened, or I might take something personally and then realize later I was probably just stressed. But I don’t think I’ve believed something impossible, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI’m trying not to minimize it, but I also don’t want to say yes to something that doesn’t really fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice your thoughts or speech becoming jumbled or hard to follow when you're not at work, like when you're talking to family or just thinking through something by yourself?\n\nPatient: A little bit, yeah… not as much as at work, I don’t think, or maybe I just notice it more there because people are waiting on me to be clear.\n\nBut even with family, sometimes I’ll be trying to explain something simple and I can feel myself sort of bouncing around. Like I start on one point and then I’m suddenly on another, and I can tell I’m not making it easy to follow.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I came in, I guess. At work especially, when I’m trying to explain inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, my words start jumping from trucks to Thursdays to the church grill to my boss’s tone… and people look at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. I just lose the order of it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or broadcasted so others can hear them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never had the sense that someone was putting thoughts in my head, or taking them out, or that other people could hear them.\n\nI mean, sometimes I worry I’m not explaining myself right, or that people can tell I’m scattered, but that’s different, I think. I don’t want to answer it wrong, but no, that particular thing hasn’t happened to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice your thoughts or speech becoming disorganized or hard to follow even when you’re not trying to explain something at work—like when you’re just thinking to yourself, daydreaming, or talking casually with someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nIt’s most obvious at work, like I said, when I’m trying to explain inventory numbers or the weekend schedule. My words kind of jump around—from the trucks, to Thursday, to the church grill, to my boss’s tone—and then I can see people looking at me like they’re trying to follow but can’t.\n\nOutside of work, it happens some too, but I might cover it better. If I’m just talking casually, I can lose the order of what I meant to say and have to circle back. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that turned out to be completely untrue, but at the time you were absolutely sure it was true—even if just for a short while?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, at least not something I can point to like that.\n\nI’ve been wrong about things, sure—misread a situation or thought someone meant something and then realized later I had it off. But “absolutely sure” and then it was completely untrue… I don’t know. Nothing is coming to mind.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been completely certain about something that later turned out to be impossible or completely false—like thinking someone was sending you hidden messages, or that you were someone else entirely, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like hidden messages or thinking I was somebody else.\n\nI mean, I can get worried I misunderstood someone or read too much into a look or a tone, but I don’t remember being completely certain about something impossible like that. If I’m answering the way you mean it, then no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a sudden and complete loss of your train of thought, where it felt like your mind went completely blank and you couldn't remember what you were thinking about at all, not just struggling to explain it?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like my mind goes completely blank, no… or at least I wouldn’t put it that strongly.\n\nIt’s more that when I’m trying to explain something—like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule—my thoughts don’t stay in the right lane. I start on the trucks, then I’m on Thursday, then somehow I’m talking about the church grill or my boss’s tone, and I can tell people are looking at me like they can’t follow.\n\nSo it’s not so much “blank,” I guess. More like it jumps around and I lose the thread. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Not someone controlling my thoughts or me having some special connection to things.\n\nI mean, I can get caught up in trying to understand why something happened, or I might take something personally and then realize later I was probably just stressed. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced things like hearing voices, seeing things, or feeling sensations that others don’t seem to notice, even outside of work and not just when you’re explaining something?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes if I’m tired I might think I heard my name or something, but then it’s usually just background noise or me not paying attention.\n\nI haven’t been seeing things or feeling things that other people don’t. At least not that I’ve noticed. I’m trying to be honest, but I don’t want to make something out of nothing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you suddenly lose your train of thought completely, like the words just vanish and you can’t remember what you were saying or thinking?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes, yes. I don’t know if it’s that the words completely vanish, exactly, but I’ll be trying to explain something at work, like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, and then my mind sort of jumps around.\n\nI’ll start with the trucks, then somehow I’m talking about Thursday, then the church grill, then my boss’s tone… and I can tell people are looking at me like they’re not following. I usually try to pull it back and make it sound clearer, but it’s embarrassing.\n\nSo yes, I guess there are times I lose the thread. I hope that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that most people would find very strange or impossible, even if just for a little while—like thinking someone was controlling your thoughts, or that you had a special connection to events that others couldn’t see?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had anything like that. Not someone controlling my thoughts or me having some special connection to things.\n\nI mean, I can get caught up in trying to understand why something happened, or I might take something personally and then realize later I was probably just stressed. But I don’t think I’ve believed something impossible, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI’m trying not to minimize it, but I also don’t want to say yes to something that doesn’t really fit.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice your thoughts or speech becoming jumbled or hard to follow when you're not at work, like when you're talking to family or just thinking through something by yourself?\n\nPatient: A little bit, yeah… not as much as at work, I don’t think, or maybe I just notice it more there because people are waiting on me to be clear.\n\nBut even with family, sometimes I’ll be trying to explain something simple and I can feel myself sort of bouncing around. Like I start on one point and then I’m suddenly on another, and I can tell I’m not making it easy to follow.\n\nThat’s actually part of why I came in, I guess. At work especially, when I’m trying to explain inventory numbers or the weekend schedule, my words start jumping from trucks to Thursdays to the church grill to my boss’s tone… and people look at me like they can’t follow what I’m saying. I know that sounds odd. I’m not trying to be difficult or anything. I just lose the order of it sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your thoughts are being put into your mind by someone else, taken out of your mind, or broadcasted so others can hear them?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never had the sense that someone was putting thoughts in my head, or taking them out, or that other people could hear them.\n\nI mean, sometimes I worry I’m not explaining myself right, or that people can tell I’m scattered, but that’s different, I think. I don’t want to answer it wrong, but no, that particular thing hasn’t happened to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever notice your thoughts or speech becoming disorganized or hard to follow even when you’re not trying to explain something at work—like when you’re just thinking to yourself, daydreaming, or talking casually with someone?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think so, though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nIt’s most obvious at work, like I said, when I’m trying to explain inventory numbers or the weekend schedule. My words kind of jump around—from the trucks, to Thursday, to the church grill, to my boss’s tone—and then I can see people looking at me like they’re trying to follow but can’t.\n\nOutside of work, it happens some too, but I might cover it better. If I’m just talking casually, I can lose the order of what I meant to say and have to circle back. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that turned out to be completely untrue, but at the time you were absolutely sure it was true—even if just for a short while?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t think so, at least not something I can point to like that.\n\nI’ve been wrong about things, sure—misread a situation or thought someone meant something and then realized later I had it off. But “absolutely sure” and then it was completely untrue… I don’t know. Nothing is coming to mind.\n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been completely certain about something that later turned out to be impossible or completely false—like thinking someone was sending you hidden messages, or that you were someone else entirely, even if just for a short time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like hidden messages or thinking I was somebody else.\n\nI mean, I can get worried I misunderstood someone or read too much into a look or a tone, but I don’t remember being completely certain about something impossible like that. If I’m answering the way you mean it, then no.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a sudden and complete loss of your train of thought, where it felt like your mind went completely blank and you couldn't remember what you were thinking about at all, not just struggling to explain it?\n\nPatient: Not exactly like my mind goes completely blank, no… or at least I wouldn’t put it that strongly.\n\nIt’s more that when I’m trying to explain something—like the inventory numbers or the weekend schedule—my thoughts don’t stay in the right lane. I start on the trucks, then I’m on Thursday, then somehow I’m talking about the church grill or my boss’s tone, and I can tell people are looking at me like they can’t follow.\n\nSo it’s not so much “blank,” I guess. More like it jumps around and I lose the thread. Is that the distinction you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something strongly that wasn’t true and couldn’t be explained by your cultural or religious background—like thinking someone was secretly in love with you, or that you had a serious illness when doctors said you didn’t?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so… not in that way.\n\nI mean, I’ve worried about my health before like anyone does, or wondered if someone liked me, but not where I was convinced and then doctors or other people proved it couldn’t be true. I wouldn’t want to make it sound like more than it was.\n\nSo I think the answer is no, unless I’m misunderstanding what you’re asking."
